34. Definition of Preventive Maintenance           35. General Preventive Maintenance

    Preventive maintenance is a systematic series           Techniques
of operations performed periodically on equip-
ment to maintain top efficiency in performance,        a. Use No. 0000 sandpaper to remove cor-
to minimize unwanted interruptions in service,     rosion.
and to eliminate major breakdowns. To under-
stand what is meant by preventive mainte-              b. Use a clean, dry, lint-free cloth or a dry
nance, it is necessary to distinguish preventive   brush for cleaning.
maintenance from trouble shooting and repair.
The primary function of preventive mainte-                 (1) If necessary, except for electrical con-
nance is to prevent breakdowns and the need                       tacts, moisten or brush with Solvent,
for repair. On the other hand, the primary                       dry-cleaning (SD); then pine the
function of trouble shooting and repair is to                    parts with a dry cloth.
locate and correct existing defects. Preventive
maintenance is of utmost importance. The use-              (2) Clean electrical contacts with a cloth
fulness of the entire system of communication                     moistened with carbon tetrachloride;
depends on equipment being ready to operate
at peak efficiency when needed.                                 then wipe them dry with a cloth, and

                                                                 burnish them.

                                                   used at a line pressure not exceeding 60 psi
                                                   (pounds per square inch) to remove dust from

                                                   mechanical damage from the air blast may

 Make connections between the equipment and          check the range on the range scale on the range-
 the power supply with insulated leads ter-
                                                     finder of the associated equipment. There should
                                                     be satisfactory performance when the range
                                                     settings are for operating between settings of
                                                     10 and 90. If the performance is not satisfac-

                                                     tory, check the adjustments associated with the

                                                     transmitting mechanisms. Let the equipment
                                                     operate for 10 minutes and again check the

                                                     range. There should be.correct transmission in

SHIPMENT AND LIMITED STORAGE AND

 DEMOLITION TO PREVENT ENEMY USE

Section I. SHIPMENT AND LIMITED STORAGE

168. Disassembly                                   transmitter distributor and receiving trans-

   Because the XD82 types of transmitter dis-      mitter distributors should be repacked in the |
tributors used with Reperforator Transmit-
ters TG—26-A, TG—26-B, and 'TG<27-A are            original individual boxes, if available. If the
mounted permanently to the base of the chest
furnished with these reperforator transmit-        original boxes are not available, place the equip-
ters, it is unnecessary to disassemble the trans-  ment in a small cardboard box or wrap it with
mitter distributor. The complete chest is packed   heavy wrapping paper. Place the box or
in one box, and repacking instructions are con-    wrapped equipment in a strong wooden box of
tained in TM 11-2201.                              appropriate size and cushion it with excelsior to
                                                   prevent shifting of the equipment. The excelsior
169. Repacking                                     should be at least 3 inches thick between the
                                                   sides of the box and all parts of the equipment.
   a. The: XD86 type transmitter distributors      Nail the cover of the box securely and strap it
used with fixed plant equipment and replace-       with metal tape or strong wire.

ment XD82 type transmitter distributors for           b. Mark repacked equipment clearly on the
use with Reperforator Transmitters TG—26-A,        outside of the box to show the equipment con-
TG-—26-B, and TG-—27-A, the two-channel type
                                                   tained, condition of the equipment, and whether

                                                   the equipment has been moistureproofed and

                                                   fungiproofed and if so, the date.

Section Il. .DEMOLITION TO PREVENT ENEMY USE

170. Methods of Demolition                         all equipment to prevent its being used or sal-
                                                   vaged by the enemy.
   a. ,SMASH. Use sledges, axes, handaxes, pick-
axes, hammers, crowbars, heavy tools.                 a. Smash (par. 170a) the relays, capacitors,

   b. Cut. Use axes, handaxes, machetes.           switches, nameplates, and other identifying
   c. BURN. Use gasoline, kerosene, oil, flame     marks.
throwers, incendiary grenades.
   d. EXPLODE. Use firearms, grenades, TNT.           b. Cut (par. 170b) all wiring, cords: and
   e. DISPOSE. Bury in slit trenches, fox holes,
other holes. Throw in streams. Scatter.            cables, relays, and transformer windings.
   f. OTHER. Use anything immediately avail-          c. Burn (par. 170c) technical manuals, dia-
able for destruction of this equipment.
                                                   grams, and circuit labels.
171. Destruction of Components                        d. Bury or scatter (par. 170e) any or all of

                                             APPENDIX II

                            IDENTIFICATION TABLES OF PARTS

      Note. The fact that a part is listed in the tables in paragraphs 2 through 6 of this appendix is not sufficient
basis for requisitioning the item. Requisitions must cite an authorized basis, such as T/O&E, T/A, SIG 7-8-10,
SIG 10, list of allowances of expendable material, or another authorized supply basis. The Department of the Army
Supply Catalogs applicable to the equipments covered in this manual are SIG 7&8, TT-21/FG, TT-25/FG, TT-
26/FG, and TT-52/FG. For an index of available supply catalogs in the Signal portion of the Department of the
Army Supply Catalog, see the latest issue of SIG 1.
